### SOURCE, PROGRESSION AND CURRENT-LINKAGE AUDIT

- **Generation date:** 2026-09-02.

- **OCR evidence:** Repository Markdown was primary. The OCR-searchable official General Studies question papers held under books\mains and books\more_previous_papers were read only to confirm the printed wording of routed Mains demands. No official answer key, marking scheme, page precision or unsupported quotation was imported from them.
- **Qdrant:** not used; repository Markdown and available OCR context were sufficient.
- **PYQ integrity:** Three General Studies Mains demands and two objective demands are routed to this topic in the audited routing ledgers, and each Mains demand is reproduced below as a demand card with its printed year, paper, question number, directive, marks and word limit exactly as the ledgers record them: 2018 General Studies Paper II Question 17, 2019 General Studies Paper II Question 7 and 2024 General Studies Paper II Question 7. Two objective demands are also routed, namely 2018 Prelims General Studies Paper I Question 25 on the beneficiary categories and provisions of the National Food Security Act, 2013 and 2023 Prelims General Studies Paper I Question 52 on the interventions and target groups of the Anaemia Mukt Bharat strategy; the audited ledger records that the official 2018-2023 Prelims keys are not held locally, so no option, key or inferred answer is recorded for either objective demand and neither is converted into a solved question. The locally held OCR-searchable official General Studies papers were read only to confirm the printed wording of the routed Mains demands; no question was invented from them and no marking scheme or page precision was imported. Every model solution below is original work written to the repository's examiner-grade standard using the claim, named evidence, analysis and qualification pattern.
- **Live-link boundary:** One live official source was verified for this topic on 2026-09-02. The Department of Food and Public Distribution's National Food Security Act portal page was read successfully on that date and states that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013 and marks a paradigm shift in the approach to food security from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system so that about two-thirds of the population is covered, that the eldest woman of the household aged eighteen or above is mandated to be the head of the household for the purpose of issuing ration cards, that the Act is implemented in all States and Union Territories and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present with identification treated as a continuous process of excluding ineligible, fake and duplicate cards and of including births and genuine left-out households, that a guiding principle is the life-cycle approach under which pregnant women, lactating mothers and children from six months to fourteen years are entitled to a nutritious meal free of cost through anganwadi centres under the Integrated Child Development Services scheme and through schools under the mid-day meal scheme with higher nutritional norms for malnourished children up to six years, that pregnant women and lactating mothers are further entitled to cash maternity benefit of not less than six thousand rupees, that on non-supply of entitled quantities the entitled person may receive a food security allowance from the concerned State Government governed through the Food Security Allowance Rules, 2015, and that responsibility is joint, with the Centre allocating and transporting foodgrains to designated depots and assisting delivery to the fair-price shop doorstep while States and Union Territories identify eligible households, issue ration cards and distribute entitlements. Only those statements of statutory design, coverage and responsibility are used from that page. The remaining dated elements are those already carried by the repository owners with their status checks, namely the statutory Central Issue Price ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two for wheat and one for coarse grains, the merger of the welfare grain scheme into the Act from 1 January 2023 making foodgrains free of cost to Antyodaya and priority households, the Union Cabinet extension of free distribution for five years through December 2028, the administration of Poshan 2.0 by the Ministry of Women and Child Development and of PM POSHAN by the Ministry of Education, and the National Family Health Survey values of 35.5 per cent under-five stunting, 19.3 per cent wasting and 67.1 per cent child anaemia in the fifth round of 2019-21 against 29.3 per cent under-five stunting in the sixth round of 2023-24, released on 29 May 2026 and covering 715 districts and about 6.79 lakh households. Every one of those values is round-specific or policy-date-specific and is cited here with that status, and the package states expressly that rounds must never be merged and that a portability transaction count is not asserted because it rises continuously. No poverty headcount, no multidimensional poverty value, no Periodic Labour Force Survey or National Sample Survey figure, no NITI Aayog or Sustainable Development Goal score, no budget or subsidy number, no scheme eligibility rule beyond the Act's own recorded coverage rule and no official answer key is asserted or inferred anywhere in this package.
- **Fact/inference discipline:** no current-affairs item, PYQ wording, figure or quotation is invented.

### SESSION 2 — FOUNDATION — THE POVERTY AND MALNUTRITION VICIOUS CYCLE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** The cycle recorded in the owner runs from income and asset deprivation to food insecurity, from food insecurity to undernutrition in the form of both calorie deficit and protein and micronutrient deficit, from undernutrition to impaired cognitive, physical and immune development, from impaired development to low human-capital formation in schooling attainment and productivity, and from low productivity back to low earnings, which reproduces the deprivation in the next generation; naming the loop and the point of intergenerational transmission is what converts a scheme list into the explanation that the 2024 demand asks for.

**Technical definition:** Evidence chain: The poverty, malnutrition and human-capital vicious cycle Qualified use: Supply the explanation that the 2024 demand asks for instead of a scheme list.

### SESSION 3 — FOUNDATION — FOOD SECURITY AGAINST NUTRITION SECURITY

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Food security is reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food to meet dietary needs and is addressed in India primarily through the statutory foodgrain entitlement, which is a calorie and grain-access instrument, whereas nutrition security goes beyond calorie access to include dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health and sanitation environment and is therefore multi-sectoral; the examinable consequence is that the public distribution system can be working exactly as designed while nutrition outcomes stagnate, so the two must never be argued as one.

**Technical definition:** Evidence chain: Food security against nutrition security Qualified use: Protect an answer from the commonest conflation in this topic.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Evidence chain: Food security against nutrition security Qualified use: Protect an answer from the commonest conflation in this topic.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **FOUNDATION**
- **Food security against nutrition security**
- **Evidence chain**
- **Qualified use**
- **Food security**
- **Food**

**How to use them:** Frame the answer through FOUNDATION; define Food security against nutrition security, connect Evidence chain with Qualified use to explain the mechanism, and use Food security for the decisive comparison or qualification.

#### VISUAL FIRST

```text
FOOD SECURITY AGAINST NUTRITION SECURITY
01. Food security against nutrition security
BOUNDARY -> The distribution system can work exactly as designed while nutrition outcomes stagnate.
```

*This topic-specific rail fixes the evidence sequence and its exam boundary before analysis.*

### SESSION 4 — CORE — STUNTING, WASTING AND ANAEMIA

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Stunting is low height-for-age in children under five and reflects cumulative chronic nutritional and health deprivation, especially in the first thousand days, wasting is low weight-for-height in children under five and reflects recent or acute food shortage or illness and is therefore a crisis indicator, and anaemia is a haemoglobin deficiency arising from iron, folate or vitamin B12 shortfall that reduces oxygen-carrying capacity and is prevalent among children, adolescent girls and pregnant women; the three call for different policy responses, so treating them as interchangeable is a factual error rather than a stylistic one.

**Technical definition:** Evidence chain: Stunting, wasting and anaemia as distinct indicators Qualified use: Show indicator literacy before proposing any nutrition intervention.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Evidence chain: Stunting, wasting and anaemia as distinct indicators Qualified use: Show indicator literacy before proposing any nutrition intervention.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **wasting**
- **anaemia**
- **Evidence chain**
- **Qualified use**
- **The three indicators**

**How to use them:** Frame the answer through Stunting; define wasting, connect anaemia with Evidence chain to explain the mechanism, and use Qualified use for the decisive comparison or qualification.

#### VISUAL FIRST

```text
STUNTING, WASTING AND ANAEMIA
01. Stunting, wasting and anaemia as distinct indicators
BOUNDARY -> The three indicators are not interchangeable and demand different responses.
```

*This topic-specific rail fixes the evidence sequence and its exam boundary before analysis.*

Stunting is low height-for-age in children under five and reflects cumulative chronic nutritional and health deprivation, especially in the first thousand days, wasting is low weight-for-height in children under five and reflects recent or acute food shortage or illness and is therefore a crisis indicator, and anaemia is a haemoglobin deficiency arising from iron, folate or vitamin B12 shortfall that reduces oxygen-carrying capacity and is prevalent among children, adolescent girls and pregnant women; the three call for different policy responses, so treating them as interchangeable is a factual error rather than a stylistic one.

- Stunting is low height-for-age in children under five and reflects cumulative chronic nutritional and health deprivation, especially in the first thousand days, wasting is low weight-for-height in children under five and reflects recent or acute food shortage or illness and is therefore a crisis indicator, and anaemia is a haemoglobin deficiency arising from iron, folate or vitamin B12 shortfall that reduces oxygen-carrying capacity and is prevalent among children, adolescent girls and pregnant women; the three call for different policy responses, so treating them as interchangeable is a factual error rather than a stylistic one.

### SESSION 5 — CORE — THE STATUTORY ENTITLEMENT AND ITS OFFICIAL COVERAGE RULE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** The National Food Security Act, 2013 provides a statutory entitlement of five kilograms of foodgrain per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households, and the Department of Food and Public Distribution's own portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013, that it marks a paradigm shift from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system, that it is implemented in all States and Union Territories, and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present, with identification treated as a continuous process of exclusion and inclusion.

**Technical definition:** Evidence chain: The statutory entitlement under the National Food Security Act, 2013 Qualified use: Cite the statutory entitlement with its official coverage discipline attached.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Evidence chain: The statutory entitlement under the National Food Security Act, 2013 Qualified use: Cite the statutory entitlement with its official coverage discipline attached.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **The statutory entitlement**
- **its official coverage rule**
- **Evidence chain**
- **Qualified use**
- **The National Food Security Act**
- **Antyodaya Anna Yojana**

**How to use them:** Frame the answer through The statutory entitlement; define its official coverage rule, connect Evidence chain with Qualified use to explain the mechanism, and use The National Food Security Act for the decisive comparison or qualification.

```

The National Food Security Act, 2013 provides a statutory entitlement of five kilograms of foodgrain per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households, and the Department of Food and Public Distribution's own portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013, that it marks a paradigm shift from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system, that it is implemented in all States and Union Territories, and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present, with identification treated as a continuous process of exclusion and inclusion.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE AND MECHANISM

### SESSION 6 — CORE — PRICE CEILING AGAINST FREE DISTRIBUTION

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two rupees per kilogram for wheat and one rupee per kilogram for coarse grains, but since 1 January 2023 the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana has been merged with the National Food Security Act and all Act foodgrains are distributed free of cost to Antyodaya Anna Yojana and priority-household beneficiaries, a policy the Union Cabinet extended for five years through December 2028; the discipline this demands is exact, because the statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts and stating the ceiling as the price actually paid is the single commonest error in this topic.

**Technical definition:** The statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two rupees per kilogram for wheat and one rupee per kilogram for coarse grains, but since 1 January 2023 the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana has been merged with the National Food Security Act and all Act foodgrains are distributed free of cost to Antyodaya Anna Yojana and priority-household beneficiaries, a policy the Union Cabinet extended for five years through December 2028; the discipline this demands is exact, because the statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts and stating the ceiling as the price actually paid is the single commonest error in this topic.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **Price ceiling against free distribution**
- **Evidence chain**
- **Qualified use**
- **The Act**
- **Central Issue Prices**
- **Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna**

**How to use them:** Frame the answer through Price ceiling against free distribution; define Evidence chain, connect Qualified use with The Act to explain the mechanism, and use Central Issue Prices for the decisive comparison or qualification.

#### VISUAL FIRST

```text
PRICE CEILING AGAINST FREE DISTRIBUTION
01. Central Issue Price ceiling against the free-distribution policy
BOUNDARY -> The statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts.
```

*This topic-specific rail fixes the evidence sequence and its exam boundary before analysis.*

#### CORE EXPLANATION

The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two rupees per kilogram for wheat and one rupee per kilogram for coarse grains, but since 1 January 2023 the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana has been merged with the National Food Security Act and all Act foodgrains are distributed free of cost to Antyodaya Anna Yojana and priority-household beneficiaries, a policy the Union Cabinet extended for five years through December 2028; the discipline this demands is exact, because the statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts and stating the ceiling as the price actually paid is the single commonest error in this topic.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE AND MECHANISM

- The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two rupees per kilogram for wheat and one rupee per kilogram for coarse grains, but since 1 January 2023 the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana has been merged with the National Food Security Act and all Act foodgrains are distributed free of cost to Antyodaya Anna Yojana and priority-household beneficiaries, a policy the Union Cabinet extended for five years through December 2028; the discipline this demands is exact, because the statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts and stating the ceiling as the price actually paid is the single commonest error in this topic.

#### EXAMINER CAUTION

- The statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts.

### SESSION 7 — CORE — THE LIFE-CYCLE APPROACH AND THE MATERNITY ENTITLEMENT

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** The nutritious meal and the maternity benefit are provisions of the Act, not add-on schemes.

```

*This topic-specific rail fixes the evidence sequence and its exam boundary before analysis.*

#### CORE EXPLANATION

The Department of Food and Public Distribution's portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that a guiding principle of the Act is its life-cycle approach, under which special provisions entitle pregnant women, lactating mothers and children from six months to fourteen years to receive a nutritious meal free of cost through the anganwadi network under the Integrated Child Development Services scheme and through schools under the mid-day meal scheme, that higher nutritional norms are prescribed for malnourished children up to six years of age, and that pregnant women and lactating mothers are entitled to cash maternity benefit of not less than six thousand rupees to partly compensate wage loss and supplement nutrition; the same source records that the eldest woman of the household aged eighteen or above is mandated to be the head of the household for the purpose of issuing ration cards.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE AND MECHANISM

- The Department of Food and Public Distribution's portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that a guiding principle of the Act is its life-cycle approach, under which special provisions entitle pregnant women, lactating mothers and children from six months to fourteen years to receive a nutritious meal free of cost through the anganwadi network under the Integrated Child Development Services scheme and through schools under the mid-day meal scheme, that higher nutritional norms are prescribed for malnourished children up to six years of age, and that pregnant women and lactating mothers are entitled to cash maternity benefit of not less than six thousand rupees to partly compensate wage loss and supplement nutrition; the same source records that the eldest woman of the household aged eighteen or above is mandated to be the head of the household for the purpose of issuing ration cards.

### SESSION 9 — CORE — JOINT CENTRE AND STATE RESPONSIBILITY

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** The official portal records that the Act defines a joint responsibility, under which the Centre allocates the required foodgrains to States and Union Territories, transports them up to designated depots in each State and Union Territory and provides central assistance for delivery from designated Food Corporation of India godowns to the doorstep of the fair-price shop, while States and Union Territories are responsible for effective implementation, which includes identifying eligible households, issuing ration cards and distributing the foodgrain entitlement; this division is the reason why a delivery failure must be attributed to a named tier rather than to the government in general.

**Technical definition:** Evidence chain: The joint Centre and State responsibility under the Act Qualified use: Locate accountability precisely in a delivery-failure answer.

### SESSION 10 — CORE — PORTABILITY THROUGH ONE NATION ONE RATION CARD

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** One Nation One Ration Card enables portability of the Act's entitlements across States using Aadhaar-seeded ration cards and electronic point-of-sale machines at fair-price shops, with the technical backbone provided through the national informatics and food-corporation systems, and it is the instrument that addresses the exclusion of migrant and mobile beneficiaries from State-specific lists; it is not a separate ration card and not a separate entitlement but a system interoperability feature, and the owners record that its cumulative transaction count on the integrated management portal rises continuously and is therefore not fixed here, the durable fact being operational inter-State portability.

**Technical definition:** Evidence chain: Portability and the One Nation One Ration Card mechanism Qualified use: Link migrant inclusion to a named mechanism with its claim limit.

### SESSION 11 — CORE — POSHAN 2.0 AND PM POSHAN AS TWO LAYERS

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Poshan 2.0, also carried as Saksham Anganwadi, is the umbrella nutrition programme administered by the Ministry of Women and Child Development that merges the Integrated Child Development Services and related schemes and delivers supplementary nutrition as take-home ration and hot cooked meals, growth monitoring through weight and height tracking, pre-school education, immunisation referral and health and nutrition counselling through anganwadi centres, with its primary focus on children under six and on pregnant and lactating women and an explicit emphasis on the first thousand days from conception to age two.

**Technical definition:** Evidence chain: Poshan 2.0 and the anganwadi delivery layer - PM POSHAN and the school nutrition layer Qualified use: Show the age-wise division of the nutrition service architecture.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Evidence chain: Poshan 2.0 and the anganwadi delivery layer - PM POSHAN and the school nutrition layer Qualified use: Show the age-wise division of the nutrition service architecture.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **Poshan 2.0**
- **PM POSHAN as two layers**
- **Evidence chain**
- **Qualified use**
- **Poshan 2.0, also carried as Saksham Anganwadi,**
- **Poshan**

**How to use them:** Frame the answer through Poshan 2.0; define PM POSHAN as two layers, connect Evidence chain with Qualified use to explain the mechanism, and use Poshan 2.0, also carried as Saksham Anganwadi, for the decisive comparison or qualification.

#### VISUAL FIRST

```text
POSHAN 2.0 AND PM POSHAN AS TWO LAYERS

    |
    v
02. PM POSHAN and the school nutrition layer
BOUNDARY -> The ministry pairing is a standing objective-question trap.
```

*This topic-specific rail fixes the evidence sequence and its exam boundary before analysis.*

#### CORE EXPLANATION

Poshan 2.0, also carried as Saksham Anganwadi, is the umbrella nutrition programme administered by the Ministry of Women and Child Development that merges the Integrated Child Development Services and related schemes and delivers supplementary nutrition as take-home ration and hot cooked meals, growth monitoring through weight and height tracking, pre-school education, immunisation referral and health and nutrition counselling through anganwadi centres, with its primary focus on children under six and on pregnant and lactating women and an explicit emphasis on the first thousand days from conception to age two. PM POSHAN, the erstwhile mid-day meal programme, is administered by the Ministry of Education and implemented through State education departments and school management committees, providing a cooked meal to children in government and government-aided schools in Classes I to VIII with the stated objectives of attendance, retention and improved nutritional status; the ministry pairing is a standing objective-question trap, because Poshan 2.0 sits with the Ministry of Women and Child Development while PM POSHAN sits with the Ministry of Education, and the two are complementary layers for different age groups rather than duplicates.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE AND MECHANISM

- Poshan 2.0, also carried as Saksham Anganwadi, is the umbrella nutrition programme administered by the Ministry of Women and Child Development that merges the Integrated Child Development Services and related schemes and delivers supplementary nutrition as take-home ration and hot cooked meals, growth monitoring through weight and height tracking, pre-school education, immunisation referral and health and nutrition counselling through anganwadi centres, with its primary focus on children under six and on pregnant and lactating women and an explicit emphasis on the first thousand days from conception to age two.
- PM POSHAN, the erstwhile mid-day meal programme, is administered by the Ministry of Education and implemented through State education departments and school management committees, providing a cooked meal to children in government and government-aided schools in Classes I to VIII with the stated objectives of attendance, retention and improved nutritional status; the ministry pairing is a standing objective-question trap, because Poshan 2.0 sits with the Ministry of Women and Child Development while PM POSHAN sits with the Ministry of Education, and the two are complementary layers for different age groups rather than duplicates.

#### EXAMINER CAUTION

- The ministry pairing is a standing objective-question trap.

### SESSION 12 — CORE — THE FIRST THOUSAND DAYS AND THE SEQUENCING ARGUMENT

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Evidence chain: The first thousand days as the critical window Qualified use: Explain why maternal and adolescent nutrition are child-nutrition instruments.

**Technical definition:** The window from conception to the age of two is the period during which nutritional deprivation causes irreversible stunting and cognitive impairment, so interventions placed inside it yield the highest return, and this is the reason the nutrition mission is designed around pregnancy, lactation and the child under two rather than around the school-age child alone; in an answer, the window supplies the sequencing argument, because it explains why adolescent-girl and maternal nutrition are treated as child-nutrition instruments rather than as separate welfare heads.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Evidence chain: The first thousand days as the critical window Qualified use: Explain why maternal and adolescent nutrition are child-nutrition instruments.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **The first thousand days**
- **the sequencing argument**
- **Evidence chain**
- **Qualified use**
- **Qualified**

**How to use them:** Frame the answer through The first thousand days; define the sequencing argument, connect Evidence chain with Qualified use to explain the mechanism, and use Qualified for the decisive comparison or qualification.

#### VISUAL FIRST

```text
THE FIRST THOUSAND DAYS AND THE SEQUENCING ARGUMENT
01. The first thousand days as the critical window
BOUNDARY -> Deprivation inside the window causes irreversible stunting and cognitive impairment.
```

*This topic-specific rail fixes the evidence sequence and its exam boundary before analysis.*

#### CORE EXPLANATION

The window from conception to the age of two is the period during which nutritional deprivation causes irreversible stunting and cognitive impairment, so interventions placed inside it yield the highest return, and this is the reason the nutrition mission is designed around pregnancy, lactation and the child under two rather than around the school-age child alone; in an answer, the window supplies the sequencing argument, because it explains why adolescent-girl and maternal nutrition are treated as child-nutrition instruments rather than as separate welfare heads.

#### NAMED EVIDENCE AND MECHANISM

- The window from conception to the age of two is the period during which nutritional deprivation causes irreversible stunting and cognitive impairment, so interventions placed inside it yield the highest return, and this is the reason the nutrition mission is designed around pregnancy, lactation and the child under two rather than around the school-age child alone; in an answer, the window supplies the sequencing argument, because it explains why adolescent-girl and maternal nutrition are treated as child-nutrition instruments rather than as separate welfare heads.

#### EXAMINER CAUTION

- Deprivation inside the window causes irreversible stunting and cognitive impairment.

- **Mains:** Explain why maternal and adolescent nutrition are child-nutrition instruments.

- **Qualified use:** Explain why maternal and adolescent nutrition are child-nutrition instruments.

### SESSION 13 — CORE SYNTHESIS — NUTRITION-SPECIFIC AND NUTRITION-SENSITIVE CONVERGENCE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Evidence chain: Nutrition-specific against nutrition-sensitive interventions - Take-home ration against the hot cooked meal Qualified use: Build the convergence argument on a stated distinction rather than a slogan.

**Technical definition:** Nutrition-specific interventions directly target nutritional outcomes through supplementary feeding, micronutrient supplementation, growth monitoring and breastfeeding promotion, whereas nutrition-sensitive interventions address the underlying determinants through other sectors, including water, sanitation and hygiene, agricultural diversification, social protection and women's education, and act on nutrition indirectly; the convergence argument in a Mains answer rests entirely on this distinction, because it explains why stunting reduction cannot be procured from the food ministry alone.

#### CORE EXPLANATION

Nutrition-specific interventions directly target nutritional outcomes through supplementary feeding, micronutrient supplementation, growth monitoring and breastfeeding promotion, whereas nutrition-sensitive interventions address the underlying determinants through other sectors, including water, sanitation and hygiene, agricultural diversification, social protection and women's education, and act on nutrition indirectly; the convergence argument in a Mains answer rests entirely on this distinction, because it explains why stunting reduction cannot be procured from the food ministry alone. A take-home ration is a dry ration given to the beneficiary to cook at home and is used for pregnant and lactating women and for children between six months and three years, whereas a hot cooked meal is served at the anganwadi centre or the school, so the hot meal guarantees consumption at the point of delivery but requires kitchen and staffing infrastructure, while the take-home ration is more flexible and may be redistributed within the household; naming this trade-off is how an answer explains a delivery design choice rather than merely listing it.

### SESSION 14 — CORE SYNTHESIS — ACUTE MALNUTRITION AND THE TREATMENT PATH

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Severe acute malnutrition is defined by weight-for-height below minus three standard deviations, is severe wasting and requires facility-based therapeutic care at a nutrition rehabilitation centre, whereas moderate acute malnutrition lies between minus two and minus three standard deviations and can be managed through community-based supplementary feeding, and both are tracked through anganwadi growth monitoring; the examinable point is that acute malnutrition needs screening, referral, treatment where indicated and community follow-up, so an additional foodgrain allocation is not a response to it.

**Technical definition:** Evidence chain: Severe and moderate acute malnutrition and the treatment path - Measured outcomes with their survey round attached Qualified use: Answer a clinical-nutrition demand at the level of the treatment path.

#### CORE EXPLANATION

Severe acute malnutrition is defined by weight-for-height below minus three standard deviations, is severe wasting and requires facility-based therapeutic care at a nutrition rehabilitation centre, whereas moderate acute malnutrition lies between minus two and minus three standard deviations and can be managed through community-based supplementary feeding, and both are tracked through anganwadi growth monitoring; the examinable point is that acute malnutrition needs screening, referral, treatment where indicated and community follow-up, so an additional foodgrain allocation is not a response to it. The owners record the National Family Health Survey as the primary survey source for stunting, wasting, anaemia and related household outcomes and require the round to be retained beside every value: the fifth round of 2019-21 is recorded as the dated baseline with 35.5 per cent of children under five stunted, 19.3 per cent wasted and 67.1 per cent of children aged six to fifty-nine months anaemic nationally, and the sixth round of 2023-24, released on 29 May 2026, is recorded as the current national round with under-five stunting reported at 29.3 per cent, a survey covering 715 districts and about 6.79 lakh households; the rule stated by the owners is that indicators from the two rounds must never be combined without naming each round.

### SESSION 15 — CORE SYNTHESIS — MEASURED OUTCOMES, DIVERGENCE AND HONEST OWNERSHIP

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** On ownership, three Mains demands from 2018, 2019 and 2024 and two objective demands from 2018 and 2023 are routed to this topic in the audited ledgers, the official answer keys for those objective demands are recorded by the ledgers as unavailable locally, so no option is inferred or recorded, and no poverty, nutrition or coverage figure is asserted anywhere in this package without its issuing body, survey round or policy date.

**Technical definition:** Evidence chain: The measurement debate behind the poverty-hunger divergence - The intervention bank for breaking the cycle - Inclusion, exclusion and honest question ownership Qualified use: Close with dated evidence, the measurement debate and an explicit data boundary.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

    |

    |

03. Inclusion, exclusion and honest question ownership
BOUNDARY -> Rounds must never be combined, and no official key is inferred for a routed objective demand.
```

*This topic-specific rail fixes the evidence sequence and its exam boundary before analysis.*

#### CORE EXPLANATION

Income or calorie poverty, multidimensional poverty and anthropometry measure different deprivations, so falling monetary poverty can coexist with continuing hunger and undernutrition, and this divergence is the analytical core of the demand that asks about poverty and hunger moving apart alongside shrinking social expenditure; the answer route is to separate availability, access, absorption and utilisation, because availability failures are procurement problems, access failures are entitlement and portability problems, and absorption failures are health, sanitation and disease problems that no grain allocation can reach. The owner's breaking-the-cycle levers are the first-thousand-days focus delivered through the nutrition mission and the anganwadi system, school nutrition through PM POSHAN, portability for migrants through One Nation One Ration Card, women's nutrition and anaemia reduction aimed at adolescent girls and pregnant and lactating women through the Anaemia Mukt Bharat strategy with its six-by-six-by-six design, and convergence with water, sanitation and hygiene because sanitation and safe water govern nutrient absorption; the owner also records that fortification can address selected micronutrient gaps at scale but cannot replace dietary diversity, infection control, safe water or clinical treatment. Inclusion error places non-eligible persons on distribution lists while exclusion error keeps eligible persons off them, most often through documentation, seeding, migration and disability-related barriers, and the owners record that portability and deduplication aim at both while exclusion risk for migrants and marginalised groups persists, which is why the official portal's description of identification as a continuous process of removing ineligible, fake and duplicate cards and adding births and genuine left-out households is itself examinable. On ownership, three Mains demands from 2018, 2019 and 2024 and two objective demands from 2018 and 2023 are routed to this topic in the audited ledgers, the official answer keys for those objective demands are recorded by the ledgers as unavailable locally, so no option is inferred or recorded, and no poverty, nutrition or coverage figure is asserted anywhere in this package without its issuing body, survey round or policy date.

- Income or calorie poverty, multidimensional poverty and anthropometry measure different deprivations, so falling monetary poverty can coexist with continuing hunger and undernutrition, and this divergence is the analytical core of the demand that asks about poverty and hunger moving apart alongside shrinking social expenditure; the answer route is to separate availability, access, absorption and utilisation, because availability failures are procurement problems, access failures are entitlement and portability problems, and absorption failures are health, sanitation and disease problems that no grain allocation can reach.

- Inclusion error places non-eligible persons on distribution lists while exclusion error keeps eligible persons off them, most often through documentation, seeding, migration and disability-related barriers, and the owners record that portability and deduplication aim at both while exclusion risk for migrants and marginalised groups persists, which is why the official portal's description of identification as a continuous process of removing ineligible, fake and duplicate cards and adding births and genuine left-out households is itself examinable. #### COMPLETE BASIC OWNER EVIDENCE BANK

> **Subject:** Social Justice | **Tier:** Must-Do (foundation) | **GS Paper:** GS-II;
> GS-III (food security economics), GS-I (population/poverty geography).
> **Core area:** NFSA entitlements; ICDS/Poshan 2.0; PM POSHAN; ONORC portability;
> food security vs nutrition security; stunting, wasting, anaemia.
> **Grounded in:** National Food Security Act, 2013; Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna
> Yojana merger with NFSA (free foodgrains from 1 Jan 2023, extended to Dec 2028);
> ICDS/Saksham Anganwadi/Poshan 2.0 (MWCD); PM POSHAN (Ministry of Education);
> NFHS-6 (2023-24), released on 29 May 2026, with NFHS-5 as a dated comparator;
> 2024 GS-II PYQ Q7.
> ✅ = source-grounded | ⚠️ = analytical inference | 📰 = current anchor.
> *Companion: `advanced/02_Poverty-Hunger-Food-and-Nutrition-Security.md`.*

#### 1. Visual foundation

```text
         POVERTY-MALNUTRITION-HUMAN CAPITAL VICIOUS CYCLE
                         (2024 PYQ Q7)
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │                                                 │
        v                                                 │
     POVERTY ────> FOOD INSECURITY ────> UNDERNUTRITION  │
    (income/              |                    │          │
     asset                |                    │          │
     deprivation)         v                    v          │
                   calorie deficit      protein/micro-    │
                   (hunger)             nutrient deficit  │
                                              │           │
                          +-------------------+           │
                          |                               │
                          v                               │
              IMPAIRED DEVELOPMENT                        │
              - cognitive (learning)                      │
              - physical (stunting, wasting)              │
              - health (anaemia, immunity)                │
                          |                               │
                          v                               │
              LOW HUMAN CAPITAL FORMATION                 │
              (education attainment, productivity)        │
                          |                               │
                          v                               │
              LOW EARNINGS / PRODUCTIVITY ────────────────┘
              (intergenerational transmission)

        BREAKING-THE-CYCLE LEVERS
        ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ 1. First-1000-days focus (Poshan 2.0, ICDS)    │
        │ 2. School nutrition (PM POSHAN)                │
        │ 3. Portability for migrants (ONORC)            │
        │ 4. Women's nutrition & anaemia reduction       │
        │ 5. WASH convergence (sanitation-nutrition)     │
        └────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Core proposition:** ✅ The poverty-malnutrition-human-capital vicious cycle (2024
PYQ Q7) traps households across generations; breaking it requires multi-sectoral
intervention — food entitlements (NFSA), nutrition services (Poshan 2.0, PM POSHAN),
portability (ONORC), women's anaemia focus and WASH convergence — not PDS reform
alone.

#### 2. Essential definitions

| Concept | Exam-ready meaning |
|---|---|
| ✅ **Food security** | Reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food to meet dietary needs — the NFSA 2013 primarily addresses calorie/grain access through statutory foodgrain entitlements (currently distributed free of cost). |
| ✅ **Nutrition security** | Goes beyond calorie access to include dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health/sanitation environment — multi-sectoral, not just PDS. |
| ✅ **Stunting (chronic undernutrition)** | Low height-for-age in children under 5; reflects cumulative nutritional and health deprivation (especially first 1000 days) — a chronic indicator. |
| ✅ **Wasting (acute undernutrition)** | Low weight-for-height in children under 5; reflects recent/acute food shortage or illness — a crisis indicator. |
| ✅ **Anaemia** | Haemoglobin deficiency (iron, folate, B12) affecting oxygen-carrying capacity; prevalent among children, adolescent girls and pregnant women — linked to productivity and maternal/child health outcomes. |
| ✅ **NFSA, 2013** | The National Food Security Act, 2013, providing statutory entitlement to foodgrains: 5 kg/person/month for priority households, 35 kg/household/month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana (AAY) households. The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of ₹3/kg rice, ₹2/kg wheat and ₹1/kg coarse grains — but since 1 January 2023 the PM Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana (PMGKAY) has been merged with the NFSA and all NFSA foodgrains are distributed **free of cost** to AAY/priority-household beneficiaries; the Union Cabinet extended this free-foodgrain policy for five years, through December 2028. Current policy is free distribution, not the ₹1-3/kg statutory ceiling. |
| ✅ **ONORC** | One Nation One Ration Card — enables portability of NFSA entitlements across states using Aadhaar-seeded ration cards and ePoS (electronic Point of Sale) machines; critical for migrant/mobile beneficiaries. |
| ✅ **Poshan 2.0 / Saksham Anganwadi** | The umbrella nutrition programme (MWCD) merging ICDS and related schemes; delivers supplementary nutrition, growth monitoring, pre-school education and referral through anganwadi centres; emphasises first-1000-days (conception to age 2) intervention. |
| ✅ **PM POSHAN (erstwhile Mid-Day Meal)** | The school-nutrition programme (Ministry of Education) providing cooked meals to children in government/aided schools (Classes I-VIII); objectives include attendance, retention and nutritional status improvement. |

#### 3. How the food-and-nutrition-security mechanism works

1. **NFSA entitlement architecture:** Coverage via priority households (~75% rural,
   ~50% urban) and AAY (poorest of poor); foodgrain entitlement per person per
   month; the Act's statutory Central Issue Price ceiling (₹3/kg rice, ₹2/kg wheat,
   ₹1/kg coarse grains) has been superseded in practice by free distribution since
   1 January 2023 (PMGKAY-NFSA merger, extended to December 2028) — but NFSA
   addresses calorie access, not dietary diversity.
2. **Poshan 2.0 / ICDS delivery:** Anganwadi centres provide supplementary nutrition
   (take-home ration, hot cooked meals), growth monitoring (weight/height tracking),
   pre-school education, immunisation referral and health/nutrition counselling —
   primary focus is children under 6, pregnant and lactating women.
3. **PM POSHAN (school nutrition):** Cooked mid-day meal in schools addresses
   immediate hunger, improves attendance and provides a platform for nutrition
   education — complements ICDS for school-age children.
4. **ONORC portability:** Aadhaar-seeded ration cards allow beneficiaries to draw
   entitlements from any ePoS-enabled fair-price shop nationwide — addresses
   migrant exclusion from state-specific PDS lists.
5. **WASH convergence:** Sanitation (SBM), safe water (JJM) and hygiene behaviour
   affect nutrient absorption; stunting reduction requires convergence beyond food
   alone.

#### 4. Institutions and tools

- ✅ **NFSA, 2013:** Statutory entitlement; administered by Department of Food and
  Public Distribution (MoCAFPD) at Union level, state food/civil supplies
  departments at state level.
- ✅ **Poshan 2.0 / Saksham Anganwadi:** Administered by Ministry of Women and Child
  Development (MWCD); implemented through ICDS infrastructure (anganwadi centres,
  AWWs/helpers).
- ✅ **PM POSHAN:** Administered by Ministry of Education; implemented through
  state education departments and school management committees.
- ✅ **ONORC:** Technical backbone provided by NIC/FCI; requires Aadhaar seeding,
  ePoS rollout and inter-state data sharing.
- ✅ **NFHS (National Family Health Survey):** Primary survey source for stunting,
  wasting, anaemia and related household outcomes. NFHS-6 (2023-24), released on
  29 May 2026, is the current national round; retain the round beside every value.

#### 5. Indian applications and examples

- ✅ **2024 GS-II PYQ Q7 (10 marks, 150 words):** "Poverty and malnutrition create a
  vicious cycle, adversely affecting human capital formation. What steps can be
  taken to break the cycle?" — the expected answer traces the intergenerational
  trap and lists multi-sectoral levers (Poshan 2.0/first-1000-days, PM POSHAN,
  ONORC, women's anaemia, WASH convergence).
- ⚠️ ONORC enabled migrant workers during COVID-19 lockdowns to access rations
  outside their home states — illustrates the portability rationale.
- ✅ **NFHS-5 (2019-21) baseline:** 35.5% of children under five were stunted, 19.3%
  were wasted and 67.1% of children aged 6–59 months were anaemic nationally. These
  are a dated baseline, not a current dashboard total.
- ✅ **NFHS-6 (2023-24) current anchor:** under-five stunting was reported at 29.3%;
  the survey covered 715 districts and about 6.79 lakh households. Do not combine
  NFHS-5 and NFHS-6 indicators without naming each round.

#### 6. Must-Know Facts for Prelims

- ✅ NFSA, 2013 provides 5 kg/person/month for priority households and 35 kg/
  household/month for AAY households; the Act's statutory Central Issue Price
  ceiling is ₹3/kg rice, ₹2/kg wheat and ₹1/kg coarse grains, but foodgrains have
  been distributed **free of cost** to NFSA beneficiaries since 1 January 2023
  (PMGKAY-NFSA merger), extended through December 2028.
- ✅ Stunting = height-for-age (chronic); wasting = weight-for-height (acute).
- ✅ Poshan 2.0 is administered by MWCD; PM POSHAN is administered by Ministry of
  Education.
- ✅ ONORC enables inter-state portability of NFSA entitlements using Aadhaar-seeded
  ration cards and ePoS.
- ✅ First-1000-days (conception to age 2) is the critical window for preventing
  stunting and cognitive impairment.

#### 7. UPSC traps

- ❌ NFSA addresses nutrition security comprehensively. -> NFSA primarily addresses
  calorie/grain access (food security); nutrition security requires dietary
  diversity, micronutrients, care practices and WASH — a multi-sectoral effort
  beyond PDS.
- ❌ Stunting and wasting are interchangeable terms. -> Stunting (height-for-age)
  reflects chronic deprivation; wasting (weight-for-height) reflects acute
  deprivation — different indicators, different policy responses.
- ❌ PM POSHAN is administered by MWCD. -> PM POSHAN (erstwhile Mid-Day Meal) is
  administered by the Ministry of Education; Poshan 2.0/ICDS is administered by
  MWCD.
- ❌ ONORC is a separate ration card. -> ONORC is a portability mechanism enabling
  existing NFSA beneficiaries to draw entitlements anywhere; it is not a separate
  card but a system interoperability feature.
- ❌ NFSA beneficiaries currently pay ₹1-3/kg for their foodgrain entitlement. ->
  ₹3/kg rice, ₹2/kg wheat and ₹1/kg coarse grains is the Act's statutory Central
  Issue Price ceiling, not current practice; foodgrains have been provided free of
  cost to AAY/priority-household beneficiaries since 1 January 2023, a policy the
  Union Cabinet extended through December 2028.

#### 8. 📰 Current anchor

- 📰 **Survey-status check (29 May 2026):** NFHS-6 (2023-24) is released. Its
  under-five stunting estimate is 29.3%, compared with 35.5% in NFHS-5. Retain the
  reporting round and do not mix survey rounds.
- 📰 **Free-foodgrain policy:** NFSA foodgrains have been distributed free of cost
  to AAY/priority-household beneficiaries since 1 January 2023 (PMGKAY merged
  into NFSA), and the Union Cabinet extended this free-distribution policy for
  five years, through December 2028, covering roughly 81 crore beneficiaries.
- 📰 ONORC portability transactions can be tracked on the IMPDS portal; the
  cumulative transaction count rises continuously and is not fixed here — the
  durable fact is the system's operational inter-state portability.

#### 9. PYQ application

- ✅ **2024 Q7 (10 marks):** Structure the answer as:
  1. Define the vicious cycle (poverty -> undernutrition -> impaired development
     -> low productivity -> poverty).
  2. Identify the intergenerational trap (stunted child -> lower-earning adult ->
     malnourished offspring).
  3. List breaking-the-cycle levers: (a) Poshan 2.0 / first-1000-days focus, (b)
     PM POSHAN for school-age nutrition, (c) ONORC portability for migrants, (d)
     women's nutrition and anaemia reduction (adolescent girls, PLW), (e) WASH
     convergence (sanitation-nutrition link).
  4. Conclude with the multi-sectoral, convergence imperative.

- ✅ **2025 GS-II Q16 (15 marks, 250 words):** “Inequality in the ownership pattern of
  resources is one of the major causes of poverty. Discuss in the context of ‘paradox of
  poverty’.” Use the loop: unequal
  land/assets/credit/digital access -> unequal opportunity and bargaining power -> low
  capability/income -> weak saving and asset formation -> inherited inequality. A
  critical-comment answer should test this explanation against joblessness, unequal
  public-service access and implementation failures, then distinguish redistribution
  of productive assets from consumption relief.

#### 10. Mains angles

- ⚠️ Use the vicious-cycle diagram as the default framing for any poverty-nutrition
  question — it directly maps to 2024 Q7.
- ⚠️ Distinguish food security (calorie access via NFSA) from nutrition security
  (dietary diversity, micronutrients, WASH) — a recurring exam distinction.
- ⚠️ Emphasise the first-1000-days window: interventions during pregnancy and the
  first two years yield the highest returns in preventing stunting.
- ⚠️ Link ONORC to migrant-worker inclusion — a current-affairs-friendly angle.

> **Answer thesis:** The poverty-malnutrition-human-capital vicious cycle is
> intergenerational and multi-causal; breaking it requires not only food
> entitlements (NFSA) but also nutrition services focused on the first 1000 days
> (Poshan 2.0), school nutrition (PM POSHAN), portability for migrants (ONORC),
> women's anaemia reduction and WASH convergence — a single-scheme approach is
> structurally insufficient.

#### 11. Probable questions

- ✅ **Mains (10 marks, 2024 Q7):** Poverty and malnutrition create a vicious cycle,
  adversely affecting human capital formation. What steps can be taken to break
  the cycle?
- ✅ **Mains (15 marks, 2025 GS-II Q16):** “Inequality in the ownership pattern of
  resources is one of the major causes of poverty. Discuss in the context of ‘paradox of
  poverty’.”
- ⚠️ **Prelims:** Which scheme enables inter-state portability of NFSA entitlements
  using Aadhaar-seeded ration cards — ONORC, PM POSHAN, Poshan 2.0 or NFHS?
- ⚠️ **Mains (15 marks):** "Food security is necessary but not sufficient for
  nutrition security." Discuss with reference to India's policy architecture.

#### 11A. Answer architecture (10/15/20-mark support)

##### Direct Mains demands owned by Core

- **2018 GS-II:** food availability versus human-development causes of hunger.
- **2019 GS-II:** divergence between poverty reduction and hunger. These Core routes
  supersede the older `advanced/02` pointers.

##### Evidence and argument bank

- **Lifecycle nutrition:** adolescent girls -> pregnancy and first 1,000 days ->
  breastfeeding/complementary feeding -> preschool services -> PM POSHAN.
- **SAM/MAM:** severe and moderate acute malnutrition require screening, referral,
  treatment where indicated and community follow-up; foodgrain allocation is insufficient.
- **THR versus hot meals:** Take-Home Rations support household feeding for pregnancy,
  lactation and young children; supervised hot meals support anganwadi/school consumption.
- **Anaemia Mukt Bharat:** use the 6x6x6 strategy as a multi-cause intervention bank.
- **Fortification:** can address selected micronutrient gaps at scale but cannot replace
  dietary diversity, infection control, safe water or clinical treatment.
- **Measurement:** income/calorie poverty, MPI and anthropometry measure different
  deprivations; falling monetary poverty can coexist with hunger.

**10 marks:** vicious cycle plus 2-3 named interventions. **15 marks:** separate
availability, access, absorption and utilisation and use 4-6 evidence units. **20
marks:** add measurement debate, fiscal priorities, gender/WASH/health convergence and
a graded universal-versus-targeted judgment.

> **Reasoned verdict:** Food entitlement is necessary, but hunger ends only when it is
> converted into diverse diets, maternal-child care, disease control and accountable
> local delivery.

#### 12. Study links

- ✅ Advanced companion: `advanced/02_Poverty-Hunger-Food-and-Nutrition-Security.md`.
- ✅ `Economy/basic/12_MSP-Procurement-Buffer-Stocks-PDS-and-Food-Security.md` —
  procurement/buffer-stock mechanics (Economy owns this; Social Justice applies
  entitlement access).
- ✅ `Economy/basic/23_Poverty-Inequality-Social-Sector-and-Inclusive-Growth.md` —
  macro poverty/inequality measurement (Economy owns MPI detail).
- ✅ `01_Social-Justice-Concept-Inclusion-and-Welfare-State-Framework.md` — the
  capability approach: food and nutrition inputs must convert into health and
  cognitive capability.

## BASIC MCQS / REMEDIATION

### Q1. Which statement correctly identifies What this owner holds and the demands routed to it?

A. This topic owns the food and nutrition half of the capability argument, namely the statutory foodgrain entitlement, the nutrition services that sit on top of it, portability for mobile beneficiaries and the anthropometric outcomes by which the whole architecture is judged, and its distinctive feature is a dense direct-question record: three General Studies Mains demands from 2018, 2019 and 2024 and two objective demands from 2018 and 2023 are routed to it in the audited ledgers; the macro mechanics of procurement, buffer stocks and poverty measurement belong to the Economy owner, while this topic argues entitlement, access and conversion.
B. The cycle recorded in the owner runs from income and asset deprivation to food insecurity, from food insecurity to undernutrition in the form of both calorie deficit and protein and micronutrient deficit, from undernutrition to impaired cognitive, physical and immune development, from impaired development to low human-capital formation in schooling attainment and productivity, and from low productivity back to low earnings, which reproduces the deprivation in the next generation; naming the loop and the point of intergenerational transmission is what converts a scheme list into the explanation that the 2024 demand asks for.
C. Food security is reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food to meet dietary needs and is addressed in India primarily through the statutory foodgrain entitlement, which is a calorie and grain-access instrument, whereas nutrition security goes beyond calorie access to include dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health and sanitation environment and is therefore multi-sectoral; the examinable consequence is that the public distribution system can be working exactly as designed while nutrition outcomes stagnate, so the two must never be argued as one.
D. Stunting is low height-for-age in children under five and reflects cumulative chronic nutritional and health deprivation, especially in the first thousand days, wasting is low weight-for-height in children under five and reflects recent or acute food shortage or illness and is therefore a crisis indicator, and anaemia is a haemoglobin deficiency arising from iron, folate or vitamin B12 shortfall that reduces oxygen-carrying capacity and is prevalent among children, adolescent girls and pregnant women; the three call for different policy responses, so treating them as interchangeable is a factual error rather than a stylistic one.

**Answer: A.**
**Explanation:** This topic owns the food and nutrition half of the capability argument, namely the statutory foodgrain entitlement, the nutrition services that sit on top of it, portability for mobile beneficiaries and the anthropometric outcomes by which the whole architecture is judged, and its distinctive feature is a dense direct-question record: three General Studies Mains demands from 2018, 2019 and 2024 and two objective demands from 2018 and 2023 are routed to it in the audited ledgers; the macro mechanics of procurement, buffer stocks and poverty measurement belong to the Economy owner, while this topic argues entitlement, access and conversion. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

### Q2. Which chronology card should be filed under What this owner holds and the demands routed to it?

A. The National Food Security Act, 2013 provides a statutory entitlement of five kilograms of foodgrain per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households, and the Department of Food and Public Distribution's own portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013, that it marks a paradigm shift from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system, that it is implemented in all States and Union Territories, and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present, with identification treated as a continuous process of exclusion and inclusion.

### Q7. Which option preserves the source-bounded meaning of The poverty, malnutrition and human-capital vicious cycle?

The Department of Food and Public Distribution's portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that a guiding principle of the Act is its life-cycle approach, under which special provisions entitle pregnant women, lactating mothers and children from six months to fourteen years to receive a nutritious meal free of cost through the anganwadi network under the Integrated Child Development Services scheme and through schools under the mid-day meal scheme, that higher nutritional norms are prescribed for malnourished children up to six years of age, and that pregnant women and lactating mothers are entitled to cash maternity benefit of not less than six thousand rupees to partly compensate wage loss and supplement nutrition; the same source records that the eldest woman of the household aged eighteen or above is mandated to be the head of the household for the purpose of issuing ration cards.
B. The National Food Security Act, 2013 provides a statutory entitlement of five kilograms of foodgrain per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households, and the Department of Food and Public Distribution's own portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013, that it marks a paradigm shift from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system, that it is implemented in all States and Union Territories, and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present, with identification treated as a continuous process of exclusion and inclusion.
C. The cycle recorded in the owner runs from income and asset deprivation to food insecurity, from food insecurity to undernutrition in the form of both calorie deficit and protein and micronutrient deficit, from undernutrition to impaired cognitive, physical and immune development, from impaired development to low human-capital formation in schooling attainment and productivity, and from low productivity back to low earnings, which reproduces the deprivation in the next generation; naming the loop and the point of intergenerational transmission is what converts a scheme list into the explanation that the 2024 demand asks for.
D. The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two rupees per kilogram for wheat and one rupee per kilogram for coarse grains, but since 1 January 2023 the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana has been merged with the National Food Security Act and all Act foodgrains are distributed free of cost to Antyodaya Anna Yojana and priority-household beneficiaries, a policy the Union Cabinet extended for five years through December 2028; the discipline this demands is exact, because the statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts and stating the ceiling as the price actually paid is the single commonest error in this topic.

**Answer: C.**
**Explanation:** The cycle recorded in the owner runs from income and asset deprivation to food insecurity, from food insecurity to undernutrition in the form of both calorie deficit and protein and micronutrient deficit, from undernutrition to impaired cognitive, physical and immune development, from impaired development to low human-capital formation in schooling attainment and productivity, and from low productivity back to low earnings, which reproduces the deprivation in the next generation; naming the loop and the point of intergenerational transmission is what converts a scheme list into the explanation that the 2024 demand asks for. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

### Q9. Which statement correctly identifies Food security against nutrition security?

A. Food security is reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food to meet dietary needs and is addressed in India primarily through the statutory foodgrain entitlement, which is a calorie and grain-access instrument, whereas nutrition security goes beyond calorie access to include dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health and sanitation environment and is therefore multi-sectoral; the examinable consequence is that the public distribution system can be working exactly as designed while nutrition outcomes stagnate, so the two must never be argued as one.
B. The National Food Security Act, 2013 provides a statutory entitlement of five kilograms of foodgrain per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households, and the Department of Food and Public Distribution's own portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013, that it marks a paradigm shift from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system, that it is implemented in all States and Union Territories, and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present, with identification treated as a continuous process of exclusion and inclusion.
C. Stunting is low height-for-age in children under five and reflects cumulative chronic nutritional and health deprivation, especially in the first thousand days, wasting is low weight-for-height in children under five and reflects recent or acute food shortage or illness and is therefore a crisis indicator, and anaemia is a haemoglobin deficiency arising from iron, folate or vitamin B12 shortfall that reduces oxygen-carrying capacity and is prevalent among children, adolescent girls and pregnant women; the three call for different policy responses, so treating them as interchangeable is a factual error rather than a stylistic one.
D. The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two rupees per kilogram for wheat and one rupee per kilogram for coarse grains, but since 1 January 2023 the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana has been merged with the National Food Security Act and all Act foodgrains are distributed free of cost to Antyodaya Anna Yojana and priority-household beneficiaries, a policy the Union Cabinet extended for five years through December 2028; the discipline this demands is exact, because the statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts and stating the ceiling as the price actually paid is the single commonest error in this topic.

**Answer: A.**
**Explanation:** Food security is reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food to meet dietary needs and is addressed in India primarily through the statutory foodgrain entitlement, which is a calorie and grain-access instrument, whereas nutrition security goes beyond calorie access to include dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health and sanitation environment and is therefore multi-sectoral; the examinable consequence is that the public distribution system can be working exactly as designed while nutrition outcomes stagnate, so the two must never be argued as one. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

### Q18. Which chronology card should be filed under The statutory entitlement under the National Food Security Act, 2013?

A. The Department of Food and Public Distribution's portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that a guiding principle of the Act is its life-cycle approach, under which special provisions entitle pregnant women, lactating mothers and children from six months to fourteen years to receive a nutritious meal free of cost through the anganwadi network under the Integrated Child Development Services scheme and through schools under the mid-day meal scheme, that higher nutritional norms are prescribed for malnourished children up to six years of age, and that pregnant women and lactating mothers are entitled to cash maternity benefit of not less than six thousand rupees to partly compensate wage loss and supplement nutrition; the same source records that the eldest woman of the household aged eighteen or above is mandated to be the head of the household for the purpose of issuing ration cards.

### Q21. Which statement correctly identifies Central Issue Price ceiling against the free-distribution policy?

A. The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two rupees per kilogram for wheat and one rupee per kilogram for coarse grains, but since 1 January 2023 the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana has been merged with the National Food Security Act and all Act foodgrains are distributed free of cost to Antyodaya Anna Yojana and priority-household beneficiaries, a policy the Union Cabinet extended for five years through December 2028; the discipline this demands is exact, because the statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts and stating the ceiling as the price actually paid is the single commonest error in this topic.
B. The same official portal records that where entitled quantities of foodgrains or meals are not supplied, the entitled person is entitled to receive a food security allowance from the concerned State Government, payable to each person within the time and manner prescribed by the Central Government, and that these provisions are governed through the Food Security Allowance Rules, 2015; this is the clause that makes the entitlement a rights-based claim rather than a discretionary scheme, and citing it by name is what allows an answer to argue the charity-to-claim shift with evidence instead of assertion.

D. The Department of Food and Public Distribution's portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that a guiding principle of the Act is its life-cycle approach, under which special provisions entitle pregnant women, lactating mothers and children from six months to fourteen years to receive a nutritious meal free of cost through the anganwadi network under the Integrated Child Development Services scheme and through schools under the mid-day meal scheme, that higher nutritional norms are prescribed for malnourished children up to six years of age, and that pregnant women and lactating mothers are entitled to cash maternity benefit of not less than six thousand rupees to partly compensate wage loss and supplement nutrition; the same source records that the eldest woman of the household aged eighteen or above is mandated to be the head of the household for the purpose of issuing ration cards.

**Answer: A.**

### Q29. Which statement correctly identifies The food security allowance as the statutory remedy?

A. The same official portal records that where entitled quantities of foodgrains or meals are not supplied, the entitled person is entitled to receive a food security allowance from the concerned State Government, payable to each person within the time and manner prescribed by the Central Government, and that these provisions are governed through the Food Security Allowance Rules, 2015; this is the clause that makes the entitlement a rights-based claim rather than a discretionary scheme, and citing it by name is what allows an answer to argue the charity-to-claim shift with evidence instead of assertion.
B. The official portal records that the Act defines a joint responsibility, under which the Centre allocates the required foodgrains to States and Union Territories, transports them up to designated depots in each State and Union Territory and provides central assistance for delivery from designated Food Corporation of India godowns to the doorstep of the fair-price shop, while States and Union Territories are responsible for effective implementation, which includes identifying eligible households, issuing ration cards and distributing the foodgrain entitlement; this division is the reason why a delivery failure must be attributed to a named tier rather than to the government in general.
C. One Nation One Ration Card enables portability of the Act's entitlements across States using Aadhaar-seeded ration cards and electronic point-of-sale machines at fair-price shops, with the technical backbone provided through the national informatics and food-corporation systems, and it is the instrument that addresses the exclusion of migrant and mobile beneficiaries from State-specific lists; it is not a separate ration card and not a separate entitlement but a system interoperability feature, and the owners record that its cumulative transaction count on the integrated management portal rises continuously and is therefore not fixed here, the durable fact being operational inter-State portability.
D. Poshan 2.0, also carried as Saksham Anganwadi, is the umbrella nutrition programme administered by the Ministry of Women and Child Development that merges the Integrated Child Development Services and related schemes and delivers supplementary nutrition as take-home ration and hot cooked meals, growth monitoring through weight and height tracking, pre-school education, immunisation referral and health and nutrition counselling through anganwadi centres, with its primary focus on children under six and on pregnant and lactating women and an explicit emphasis on the first thousand days from conception to age two.

### Q53. Which statement correctly identifies Nutrition-specific against nutrition-sensitive interventions?

A. Nutrition-specific interventions directly target nutritional outcomes through supplementary feeding, micronutrient supplementation, growth monitoring and breastfeeding promotion, whereas nutrition-sensitive interventions address the underlying determinants through other sectors, including water, sanitation and hygiene, agricultural diversification, social protection and women's education, and act on nutrition indirectly; the convergence argument in a Mains answer rests entirely on this distinction, because it explains why stunting reduction cannot be procured from the food ministry alone.
B. Severe acute malnutrition is defined by weight-for-height below minus three standard deviations, is severe wasting and requires facility-based therapeutic care at a nutrition rehabilitation centre, whereas moderate acute malnutrition lies between minus two and minus three standard deviations and can be managed through community-based supplementary feeding, and both are tracked through anganwadi growth monitoring; the examinable point is that acute malnutrition needs screening, referral, treatment where indicated and community follow-up, so an additional foodgrain allocation is not a response to it.
C. A take-home ration is a dry ration given to the beneficiary to cook at home and is used for pregnant and lactating women and for children between six months and three years, whereas a hot cooked meal is served at the anganwadi centre or the school, so the hot meal guarantees consumption at the point of delivery but requires kitchen and staffing infrastructure, while the take-home ration is more flexible and may be redistributed within the household; naming this trade-off is how an answer explains a delivery design choice rather than merely listing it.
D. The owners record the National Family Health Survey as the primary survey source for stunting, wasting, anaemia and related household outcomes and require the round to be retained beside every value: the fifth round of 2019-21 is recorded as the dated baseline with 35.5 per cent of children under five stunted, 19.3 per cent wasted and 67.1 per cent of children aged six to fifty-nine months anaemic nationally, and the sixth round of 2023-24, released on 29 May 2026, is recorded as the current national round with under-five stunting reported at 29.3 per cent, a survey covering 715 districts and about 6.79 lakh households; the rule stated by the owners is that indicators from the two rounds must never be combined without naming each round.

**Answer: A.**
**Explanation:** Nutrition-specific interventions directly target nutritional outcomes through supplementary feeding, micronutrient supplementation, growth monitoring and breastfeeding promotion, whereas nutrition-sensitive interventions address the underlying determinants through other sectors, including water, sanitation and hygiene, agricultural diversification, social protection and women's education, and act on nutrition indirectly; the convergence argument in a Mains answer rests entirely on this distinction, because it explains why stunting reduction cannot be procured from the food ministry alone. ### Q64. Which statement avoids a close-option trap about Severe and moderate acute malnutrition and the treatment path?

A. The cycle recorded in the owner runs from income and asset deprivation to food insecurity, from food insecurity to undernutrition in the form of both calorie deficit and protein and micronutrient deficit, from undernutrition to impaired cognitive, physical and immune development, from impaired development to low human-capital formation in schooling attainment and productivity, and from low productivity back to low earnings, which reproduces the deprivation in the next generation; naming the loop and the point of intergenerational transmission is what converts a scheme list into the explanation that the 2024 demand asks for.
